Founded in 1999, the company originally focused on medical transcription and dictation services for physicians. Over time, SMARTMD expanded into a broader healthcare IT platform encompassing EHR, billing, referral management, and CRM tools, with a particular focus on the hospice, palliative care, and PACE (Program of All-Inclusive Care for the Elderly) markets.\n     9→\n    10→The company's CEO is Nandip Kothari (since 2008), who holds an MBA from Kellogg School of Management. Employee counts vary across sources — LinkedIn lists 201–500 employees, while other directories (ZoomInfo, Owler, ContactOut) suggest a smaller actual workforce of approximately 28–37 employees, suggesting the LinkedIn figure may include contracted transcriptionists or be outdated. SMARTMD was named second in the U.S. for \"Trust, Accountability, and Transparency\" by Black Book Research in 2017 for Medical Transcription. The company's specialties as listed on LinkedIn include: Medical Transcription Services, Dictation Platform, Medical Billing and Collections, EHR Scribe Services, Referral Management, MACRA/MIPS Registry, Hospice, Palliative Care, and PACE.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: SMARTMD Palliative\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 11476\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→SMARTMD Palliative is a palliative care-specific EHR and practice management system, certified as ONC Health IT Module version 6 (certified 2024-05-31). The product is described as an \"EMR, intake, and Medicare Part B\" system \"designed exclusively for palliative care\" that \"works with your existing hospice EMR.\" This positions it as a specialty-focused clinical platform intended to complement (not replace) a hospice organization's primary EMR system, while providing palliative care-specific documentation, billing, and workflow tools.\n    19→\n    20→The certified module carries a substantial set of ONC criteria: clinical data management ((a)(1), (a)(2), (a)(5), (a)(14)), transitions of care ((b)(1)), EHI export ((b)(10)), receive/display C-CDA ((b)(11)), clinical quality measures ((c)(1)), FHIR API access ((g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)), and direct messaging ((h)(1)). This breadth suggests the product functions as a fairly complete clinical EHR, not just a documentation overlay.\n    21→\n    22→SMARTMD appears to be part of a broader product ecosystem that includes:\n    23→- **SMARTMD Palliative** — the palliative care EHR (this certified product)\n    24→- **Accelerate CRM** — a hospice/palliative-specific CRM for managing referral sources and sales\n    25→- **VIP (Virtual Intake Platform)** — a mobile-friendly patient admissions/enrollment tool used for PACE and hospice\n    26→- **Medical Transcription Services** — the company's original business, available standalone or integrated\n    27→- **Medical Billing & Collections** — billing services, likely partly integrated with the EHR\n    28→\n    29→It is unclear which of these are separate products vs. modules within a single platform. The CHPL certification applies specifically to \"SMARTMD Palliative\" but the broader ecosystem likely shares data and infrastructure.\n    30→\n    31→### Users & Market\n    32→\n    33→SMARTMD Palliative targets palliative care programs, particularly those operated by or alongside hospice organizations. The product is designed for:\n    34→- **Palliative care providers** (physicians, nurse practitioners) who document patient visits\n    35→- **Social workers and chaplains** on interdisciplinary care teams\n    36→- **Administrative/billing staff** who manage chronic care billing and reimbursement\n    37→- **Field-based providers** who need mobile access for home visits\n    38→\n    39→The product emphasizes support for the CMS GUIDE (Guiding an Improved Dementia Experience) payment model, automatically generating documentation and spreadsheets formatted per CMS requirements. This suggests the customer base includes organizations participating in CMS value-based care demonstrations.\n    40→\n    41→No specific customer counts were found. The company's market appears to be small-to-mid-size hospice organizations that are adding palliative care programs and need a specialized EHR that integrates with their existing hospice EMR. The integration with Axxess Palliative Care (a major hospice platform) suggests SMARTMD serves as a complementary tool rather than a standalone enterprise system.\n    42→\n    43→### Modules & Functionality\n    44→\n    45→Based on vendor materials and search results, the following modules and features are described:\n    46→\n    47→**Clinical Documentation:**\n    48→- Clinical notes designed by palliative care providers with specialty-specific templates\n    49→- Standard palliative assessments built in: PPS (Palliative Performance Scale), FAST (Functional Assessment Staging Tool), ESAS (Edmonton Symptom Assessment System)\n    50→- Key quality measures (acuity, PPS score, pain levels) automatically extracted from notes and plotted on trend graphs to show patient capability and autosync\n    53→- Ability to scan forms, capture consent documentation, and manage files\n    54→\n    55→**Scheduling & Patient Management:**\n    56→- Provider schedules pushed to mobile phones with one-tap directions to patient homes\n    57→- Appointment scheduling at bedside; system can auto-place placeholder appointments based on clinical notes\n    58→- Patient boards with real-time status updates visible to the care team\n    59→- Dashboards for providers, admin staff, and management\n    60→\n    61→**Billing & Reimbursement:**\n    62→- Designed for chronic care billing — PCM (Principal Care Management), TCM (Transitional Care Management), CCM (Chronic Care Management), and PIN codes\n    63→- Time-tracking for patient conversations to ensure every billable minute is captured\n    64→- Custom time-based billing reports that recommend CPT codes based on chronic care coding rules\n    65→- Month-end billing generation\n    66→- Medicare Part B billing support\n    67→\n    68→**Team Communication:**\n    69→- HIPAA-compliant secure messaging platform for interdisciplinary care team communication\n    70→- Designed to support multiple providers, social workers, and chaplains on the same patient\n    71→\n    72→**Integrations:**\n    73→- Integration with Axxess Palliative Care for patient demographics, admissions, discharges, diagnoses, and referral, and admissions processes\n    81→- Account prioritization and market data to identify new referral partnerships\n    82→- Complete view of organizational relationships from initial partner contact to facility census\n    83→\n    84→**Intake & Admissions (VIP):**\n    85→- Virtual Intake Platform for fully mobile-friendly enrollment\n    86→- Supports patients, caregivers, families, and admissions teams\n    87→- Used for PACE enrollments with implementation in 4–6 weeks\n    88→\n    89→**Quality & Compliance:**\n    90→- MACRA/MIPS registry and reporting capabilities\n    91→- CMS Founded in 1999, the company originally focused on medical transcription and dictation services for physicians. Over time, SMARTMD expanded into a broader healthcare IT platform encompassing EHR, billing, referral management, and CRM tools, with a particular focus on the hospice, palliative care, and PACE (Program of All-Inclusive Care for the Elderly) markets.\n\nThe company's CEO is Nandip Kothari (since 2008), who holds an MBA from Kellogg School of Management. Employee counts vary across sources — LinkedIn lists 201–500 employees, while other directories (ZoomInfo, Owler, ContactOut) suggest a smaller actual workforce of approximately 28–37 employees, suggesting the LinkedIn figure may include contracted transcriptionists or be outdated. SMARTMD was named second in the U.S. for \"Trust, Accountability, and Transparency\" by Black Book Research in 2017 for Medical Transcription. The company's specialties as listed on LinkedIn include: Medical Transcription Services, Dictation Platform, Medical Billing and Collections, EHR Scribe Services, Referral Management, MACRA/MIPS Registry, Hospice, Palliative Care, and PACE.\n\n## Product: SMARTMD Palliative\n\nCHPL ID: 11476\n\n### What It Is\n\nSMARTMD Palliative is a palliative care-specific EHR and practice management system, certified as ONC Health IT Module version 6 (certified 2024-05-31). The product is described as an \"EMR, intake, and Medicare Part B\" system \"designed exclusively for palliative care\" that \"works with your existing hospice EMR.\" This positions it as a specialty-focused clinical platform intended to complement (not replace) a hospice organization's primary EMR system, while providing palliative care-specific documentation, billing, and workflow tools.\n\nThe certified module carries a substantial set of ONC criteria: clinical data management ((a)(1), (a)(2), (a)(5), (a)(14)), transitions of care ((b)(1)), EHI export ((b)(10)), receive/display C-CDA ((b)(11)), clinical quality measures ((c)(1)), FHIR API access ((g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10)), and direct messaging ((h)(1)). This breadth suggests the product functions as a fairly complete clinical EHR, not just a documentation overlay.\n\nSMARTMD appears to be part of a broader product ecosystem that includes:\n- **SMARTMD Palliative** — the palliative care EHR (this certified product)\n- **Accelerate CRM** — a hospice/palliative-specific CRM for managing referral sources and sales\n- **VIP (Virtual Intake Platform)** — a mobile-friendly patient admissions/enrollment tool used for PACE and hospice\n- **Medical Transcription Services** — the company's original business, available standalone or integrated\n- **Medical Billing & Collections** — billing services, likely partly integrated with the EHR\n\nIt is unclear which of these are separate products vs. modules within a single platform. The CHPL certification applies specifically to \"SMARTMD Palliative\" but the broader ecosystem likely shares data and infrastructure.\n\n### Users & Market\n\nSMARTMD Palliative targets palliative care programs, particularly those operated by or alongside hospice organizations. The product is designed for:\n- **Palliative care providers** (physicians, nurse practitioners) who document patient visits\n- **Social workers and chaplains** on interdisciplinary care teams\n- **Administrative/billing staff** who manage chronic care billing and reimbursement\n- **Field-based providers** who need mobile access for home visits\n\nThe product emphasizes support for the CMS GUIDE (Guiding an Improved Dementia Experience) payment model, automatically generating documentation and spreadsheets formatted per CMS requirements. This suggests the customer base includes organizations participating in CMS value-based care demonstrations.\n\nNo specific customer counts were found. The company's market appears to be small-to-mid-size hospice organizations that are adding palliative care programs and need a specialized EHR that integrates with their existing hospice EMR. The integration with Axxess Palliative Care (a major hospice platform) suggests SMARTMD serves as a complementary tool rather than a standalone enterprise system.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials and search results, the following modules and features are described:\n\n**Clinical Documentation:**\n- Clinical notes designed by palliative care providers with specialty-specific templates\n- Standard palliative assessments built in: PPS (Palliative Performance Scale), FAST (Functional Assessment Staging Tool), ESAS (Edmonton Symptom Assessment System)\n- Key quality measures (acuity, PPS score, pain levels) automatically extracted from notes and plotted on trend graphs to show patient trajectory toward hospice\n- Voice documentation with speech-to-text engine that transcribes and summarizes dictation\n- Mobile documentation via native iOS and Android apps with offline capability and autosync\n- Ability to scan forms, capture consent documentation, and manage files\n\n**Scheduling & Patient Management:**\n- Provider schedules pushed to mobile phones with one-tap directions to patient homes\n- Appointment scheduling at bedside; system can auto-place placeholder appointments based on clinical notes\n- Patient boards with real-time status updates visible to the care team\n- Dashboards for providers, admin staff, and management\n\n**Billing & Reimbursement:**\n- Designed for chronic care billing — PCM (Principal Care Management), TCM (Transitional Care Management), CCM (Chronic Care Management), and PIN codes\n- Time-tracking for patient conversations to ensure every billable minute is captured\n- Custom time-based billing reports that recommend CPT codes based on chronic care coding rules\n- Month-end billing generation\n- Medicare Part B billing support\n\n**Team Communication:**\n- HIPAA-compliant secure messaging platform for interdisciplinary care team communication\n- Designed to support multiple providers, social workers, and chaplains on the same patient\n\n**Integrations:**\n- Integration with Axxess Palliative Care for patient demographics, admissions, discharges, diagnoses, and location data exchange\n- Designed to work alongside existing hospice EMR systems\n- C-CDA receive/display capability (certified (b)(11))\n- FHIR API access (certified (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10))\n- Direct messaging capability (certified (h)(1))\n\n**CRM & Referral Management (Accelerate CRM):**\n- Referral source management connecting sales, referral, and admissions processes\n- Account prioritization and market data to identify new referral partnerships\n- Complete view of organizational relationships from initial partner contact to facility census\n\n**Intake & Admissions (VIP):**\n- Virtual Intake Platform for fully mobile-friendly enrollment\n- Supports patients, caregivers, families, and admissions teams\n- Used for PACE enrollments with implementation in 4–6 weeks\n\n**Quality & Compliance:**\n- MACRA/MIPS registry and reporting capabilities\n- CMS GUIDE model documentation support with auto-generated compliance spreadsheets\n- Clinical quality measure tracking (certified (c)(1))\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features and certifications described, the product manages the following data types (supported by evidence):\n\n- **Patient demographics and status** — evidenced by Axxess integration exchanging demographics, admissions, discharges, diagnoses, and locations\n- **Clinical encounter notes** — palliative-specific documentation with structured assessments (PPS, FAST, ESAS)\n- **Quality measure data** — acuity scores, PPS scores, pain levels extracted and trended over time\n- **Audio dictation recordings** — voice documentation feature with transcription (confirmed by App Store listing)\n- **Schedules and appointments** — provider scheduling with mobile access\n- **Billing/charge data** — time-based chronic care billing, CPT codes, monthly billing reports, Medicare Part B\n- **Secure messages** — HIPAA-compliant team messaging\n- **Scanned documents and images** — consent forms, scanned documents (per App Store listing)\n- **Referral source and CRM data** — if Accelerate CRM is part of the certified product ecosystem\n- **MIPS/quality reporting data** — per App Store listing and MACRA/MIPS registry capability\n- **Care plans** — implied by palliative care workflows and CMS GUIDE model support, though not explicitly described as a standalone feature\n- **C-CDA documents** — receive and display capability certified under (b)(11)\n- **FHIR resources** — exposed via certified API ((g)(10))\n\n**Gaps/Uncertainties:**\n- The website does not mention e-prescribing, which is consistent with the absence of (a)(10) certification. Palliative care may rely on the primary hospice EMR for medication management.\n- No patient portal or patient-facing view-download-transmit feature is mentioned, and (e)(1) is not among the certified criteria.\n- It is unclear whether the Accelerate CRM and VIP intake platform data are part of the certified product's data scope or are separate products.\n- The website is heavily JavaScript-rendered, making it difficult to extract detailed content; some features may be described on the site but not captured in this research.\n- Lab results, imaging, and other diagnostic data are not mentioned — palliative care programs may rely on other systems for these.\n\n---\n","numLines":120,"startLine":1,"totalLines":120}}}

V6\nINTRODUCTION\n\nIn this documentation we will list all the steps required to access protected health information based on the documentation of an open API. The\nAPI makes health information broadly available using FHIR®, a set of clinical interoperability resources under the umbrella of the HL7 standards\norganization. FHIR is based on common web standards can be reached through a RESTful protocol in which each FHIR resource has a known\nURL. This document describes OAuth 2.0 for client applications to authorize, authenticate, and integrate with FHIR-based data systems.\nWe follow Smart-On-FHIR authentication (OAuth2) and authorization (Ref. http://hl7.org/fhir/smart-app-launch/toc.html )\n\nFHIR Endpoints\nNote: Production and Test Server endpoints are subject to change.\n\nProduction Endpoint\n•\n•\n\nFHIR : https://fhir.smartmd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/\nAuthentication Server: https://oauth.smartmd.com/\n\nTest Server Endpoint\n•\n•\n\nFHIR : https://fhir.smartmd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/\nAuthentication Server: https://oauth.smartmd.com/\n\nTypes of Smart-On-FHIR applications\n•\n\nStandalone App : SMART on FHIR confidential client with a patient context, refresh token, and OpenID Connect (OIDC) identity\ntoken.\n\n•\n\nEHR Embedded app: Demonstrate the ability to perform an EHR launch to a SMART on FHIR\n\n•\n\nSmart Backend Services App (Multi-patient authorization and API) : These are server-to-server backend applications e.g. Export\nclinical data for multiple patients in a group. This app is system level app without any UI.\n\nTypes of Authentications supported\n•\n\nSymmetric (“client secret”) authentication (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Symmetric Client Auth - FHIR\nv4.0.1)\n\n•\n\nAsymmetric (“private key JWT”) authentication (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Asymmetric Client Auth\n- FHIR v4.0.1)\n\n•\n\nPublic Clients (HL7.FHIR.UV.SMART-APP-LAUNCH\\Example App Launch for Public Client - FHIR v4.0.1)\n\nSteps for Smart App Launch\n1.\n\nClient App Registration\n\n2.\n\nRetrieve .well-known/smart-configuration\n\n3.\n\nObtain authorization code\n\n4.\n\nObtain access token\n\n5.\n\nAccess FHIR API\n\n6.\n\nRefresh Token to obtain new access token\n\n\fClient APP Registration\nBefore a SMART app can run against an EHR, the app must be registered with that EHR’s authorization service. We are using OAuth 2.0 Client\nRegistration.\n\nClient App Request process\n•\n\nThird-party application must sign the API Subscription Agreement with following details\no\no\no\no\no\n\n•\n\nThe app name\nThe necessary APIs / Scopes\nAny redirect URIs\nLaunch URL (optional)\nURL to JWK Set (Only for apps supporting asymmetric client authentication).\n\nThe application is created by the EHR admin after confirming the app’s registration parameters and communicates a client_id to the\napp.\no FHIR APIs (of any supported version) listed within the USCDI v1 core data set will be supported\no Only reads data from FHIR server\n\nRetrieve .well-known/smart-configuration\nIn order to obtain launch context and request authorization to access FHIR resources, the app discovers the EHR FHIR server’s SMART\nconfiguration metadata, including OAuth authorization_endpoint and token_endpoint URLs.\nFHIR server makes SMART configuration available from well-known endpoint. You can get Authorization end point and token endpoint.\n\nRequest\nGET 200 https://fhir.smartmd.com/r4/.well-known/smart-configuration\n\nResponse\n{\n\"capabilities\": [\n\"launch-ehr\",\n\"launch-standalone\",\n\"client-public\",\n\"client-confidential-symmetric\",\n\"sso-openid-connect\",\n\"context-banner\",\n\"context-style\",\n\"context-ehr-patient\",\n\"context-ehr-encounter\",\n\"context-standalone-patient\",\n\"context-standalone-encounter\",\n\"permission-offline\",\n\"permission-patient\",\n\"permission-user\"\n],\n\"revocation_endpoint\": No login walls, no redirects.\n\n6. **Took a full-page screenshot** of the compliance page in the browser, confirming the page is a simple static layout with the links listed above. No accordions, no hidden content, no JavaScript-rendered sections beyond the basic page structure.\n\n7. **Checked EHI PDF for embedded URLs and attachments** — no embedded URLs or attachments found. The PDF is self-contained.\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### B(10) Export Documentation (Primary EHI Export Document)\n\nThe core EHI export documentation is a 10-page PDF titled \"ONC Certification 170.315(b)(10) Electronic Health Information Export, 2024v1.\" It describes a **per-patient JSON export** mechanism:\n\n**Export mechanism:** Users navigate to the Patients tab, select one or more patients, and tap the \"Export\" button. The export produces a JSON file **per patient**.\n\n**Data sections documented with field-level detail:**\n\n| Section | Fields Documented | Description |\n|---------|-------------------|-------------|\n| **Patient** | 13 fields | Demographics: PatientId, PatientName, ChartID, DOB, Case (service line), Address, City, State, Zip, Country, HomePhone, WorkPhone, MobilePhone, Email |\n| **KinList** | 10 fields | Caregivers/relatives: PatientId, KinId, FirstName, LastName, MobilePhone, Email, DeceasedFlag, Decisional, Country, RelationshipList |\n| **Meds** | 13 fields | Medications: Strength, StrengthUnit, Form, DoseQuantity, StrengthForm, StartDate, EndDate, DoseUnit, Drug, DrugId, DrugInstructions, WrittenAs, Status |\n| **Allergies** | 4 fields | Allergen, Reaction, WrittenAs, Status |\n| **Problems** | 6 fields | ClinicalSummaryProblemDetailsList: DictionaryCode (ICD-10), Problems (description), ActiveDate, ResolvedDate, ProblemId, isResolved, isPrimaryDx |\n\n**Sample JSON export** is included (pages 7–9), showing a complete patient record with all five sections populated. The sample reveals additional undocumented structures:\n\n- A **CaseList** array containing case/encounter-level data: provider information (ProviderId, ProviderName), patient location details, case descriptions (e.g., \"Hospice\"), diagnosis codes, insurance lists, external/internal contact lists, and creation timestamps.\n- The CaseList section includes a **CaseProvider** sub-object with provider identifiers and names.\n- The CaseList includes **InsuranceList**, **ExternalContactList**, and **InternalContactList** arrays (empty in the sample but present in the structure).\n\nThese CaseList fields are NOT documented in the field-level tables but ARE present in the sample JSON output.\n\nEach field has a name, data type (string, date, bool, int), and a brief description. Date formats are specified (e.g., \"m/d/yyyy\" for DOB, ISO 8601 timestamps in the sample data).\n\n### ONC Disclosure Statement\n\nA 2-page PDF listing all certified criteria for SMARTMD Palliative Version 6. Confirms (b)(10) certification. Notes that EMR Direct Interoperability Engine v2023 is relied-upon software for (b)(1) and (h)(1). Updated January 25, 2025.\n\n### FHIR API Documentation\n\nA 58-page PDF documenting the SMART on FHIR API (for g(10) certification). This covers:\n- FHIR endpoints (production: https://fhir.smartmd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/)\n- SMART on FHIR authentication (OAuth2, symmetric/asymmetric/public client flows)\n- FHIR resources: Patient, AllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition, ImplantableDevice, DiagnosticReport/ClinicalNotes/DocumentReference, Laboratory Observations, Goal, Immunization, Medication, SmokingStatus, Procedure, Provenance, VitalSigns, CCDA\n- Search operations (Patient, Encounter, Document)\n\nThis is the **g(10) standardized API** documentation, NOT the b(10) EHI export. It covers USCDI v1 data classes via FHIR R4. The b(10) export is the separate JSON-based mechanism described in the EHI export PDF.\n\n### API.json\n\nA FHIR R4 Bundle (collection) containing two Endpoint resources and two Organization resources. These are Direct messaging endpoint configurations pointing to a test/certification server (fhirserver.justtest.in), not production endpoints. This appears to be related to the (h)(1) Direct Project criterion rather than the b(10) export.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nBased on the product research, SMARTMD Palliative stores data across these domains. Here is how the b(10) export covers them:\n\n| Data Domain | Covered in Export? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Patient demographics** | Yes | 13 fields in Patient section |\n| **Next of kin / caregivers** | Yes | KinList section with contacts and decisional status |\n| **Medications** | Yes | Active medications with drug details, dosing, start/end dates |\n| **Allergies** | Yes | Drug/food/environmental allergies with reactions |\n| **Problems / Diagnoses** | Yes | ICD-10 coded, with onset/resolution dates, primary dx flag |\n| **Provider assignments** | Partial | In CaseList sample but undocumented |\n| **Case/service line info** | Partial | In CaseList sample but undocumented |\n| **Insurance** | Partial | InsuranceList array present in sample JSON but empty; no field docs |\n| **Clinical encounter notes** | **Not documented** | Core product feature — palliative care notes, dictation transcriptions |\n| **Palliative assessments (PPS, FAST, ESAS)** | **Not documented** | Specialty-specific structured assessments are a key product feature |\n| **Billing / charges / CPT codes** | **Not documented** | Time-based chronic care billing (PCM, TCM, CCM) is a core module |\n| **Scheduled appointments** | **Not documented** | Provider scheduling with mobile push |\n| **Secure messages** | **Not documented** | HIPAA-compliant team messaging |\n| **Scanned documents / images** | **Not documented** | Consent forms, scanned documents |\n| **Care plans** | **Not documented** | Implied by palliative workflows and CMS GUIDE model support |\n| **Quality measure data (PPS trends, acuity scores)** | **Not documented** | Extracted from notes and plotted as trend graphs |\n| **CRM / referral source data** | **Not documented** | May be a separate product (Accelerate CRM) |\n| **MIPS/quality reporting data** | **Not documented** | Registry and reporting capability |\n\n**The export covers approximately 5 of 15+ data domains** stored by the product. The documented export is essentially a patient demographic summary with medication, allergy, and problem lists — very similar to what a C-CDA patient summary or USCDI core dataset would include, just in JSON format.\n\n**Critical missing domains for a b(10) export:**\n\n1. **Clinical encounter notes** — This is the product's primary function. Palliative care providers create detailed clinical documentation at every visit. No notes appear in the export.\n2. **Palliative care assessments** (PPS, FAST, ESAS) — These are structured, specialty-specific clinical instruments that are central to the product. They track patient trajectory toward hospice. Their absence is a significant gap.\n3. **Billing and charge data** — The product has detailed time-based billing with CPT code recommendations. No billing data appears in the export.\n4. **Care plans** — Implied by the palliative care workflow and CMS GUIDE model support. Not in the export.\n5. **Scanned documents and images** — Consent forms and other documents mentioned in app store listings. Not in the export.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **proprietary JSON format** — not FHIR, not C-CDA, not any recognized healthcare interoperability standard. This is not inherently problematic for b(10); a vendor-specific format is acceptable as long as it includes all EHI. However:\n\n- The JSON structure is flat and simple — good for basic parsing\n- Field naming is inconsistent (camelCase mixed with PascalCase)\n- Date formats vary within the same export (e.g., \"m/d/yyyy\" vs ISO 8601 timestamps)\n- The export is per-patient, which is appropriate for individual patient requests\n- A third party could reconstruct the demographic/medication/allergy/problem data from this export, but NOT the full patient record since clinical notes, assessments, billing, and documents are absent\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n- **Readable but minimal** — The 10-page PDF is clearly formatted with tables for each section. Field names, types, and descriptions are provided.\n- **Sample data included** — A complete JSON example with all sections populated helps developers understand the format.\n- **Undocumented structures** — The sample JSON contains a CaseList structure with provider info, location data, insurance, and contacts that is NOT described in the field documentation tables. This means the actual export may be slightly broader than the documentation suggests, but the documentation is incomplete.\n- **No schema file** — There is no JSON Schema, no OpenAPI spec, no machine-readable format definition. Only the PDF tables and sample.\n- **No value sets** — The documentation doesn't define valid values for fields like \"Case\" (service line), \"Status\", or relationship types. The sample shows examples but doesn't enumerate possibilities.\n- **No versioning** — Labeled \"2024v1\" but no changelog or versioning strategy documented.\n\nA developer could implement a basic import of this data, but would need to handle the undocumented CaseList fields and guess at value constraints. The documentation is adequate for a patient summary import but insufficient for reconstructing a full patient record.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Field-level with name, type, and brief description. Better than just listing table names, but lacking constraints, cardinality, and optionality.\n- **Coded fields**: ICD-10 codes documented for Problems; RxNorm codes appear in the sample (RxNtCode field) but are not documented in the field tables. No other coded field documentation.\n- **Relationships**: Implicit via PatientId linkage across sections. No formal relationship documentation.\n- **Cardinality**: Not specified. The sample suggests arrays for KinList, Meds, Allergies, Problems, and CaseList, but min/max counts are not documented.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nSMARTMD Palliative has done the minimum to check the b(10) certification box. They have a dedicated EHI export feature (not just repurposed FHIR API), which is good — it demonstrates awareness that b(10) is separate from g(10). The JSON-based per-patient export with field documentation and sample data shows genuine effort.\n\nHowever, the export covers only the narrowest slice of patient data: demographics, medications, allergies, and problems. This is roughly equivalent to a patient summary — the same data that g(10) already covers through the FHIR API. For a palliative care EHR whose primary value is in its clinical documentation, structured assessments (PPS, FAST, ESAS), and specialty billing, the absence of notes, assessments, billing data, care plans, documents, and messages means the export captures perhaps 20-30% of the EHI the product stores.\n\nThe documentation itself is adequate for what it covers but incomplete even for the data it does export (CaseList undocumented). The lack of a machine-readable schema and the absence of value set definitions further limit its utility.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL: https://www.smartmd.com/compliance/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl; WordPress static page)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (two links directly on the compliance page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare present but no blocking)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- None. The page was straightforward, all links worked, all downloads were valid. No login walls, no JavaScript-dependent content for the EHI links.\n- The API.json file is hosted on a different domain (secure.smartmd.com) but was publicly accessible.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":395,"cache_read_input_tokens":59005,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":395},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"041b552f-64fa-4f04-9912-af3957f9ecab","uuid":"ca5c0264-bb6b-4ea0-bcca-9066ca7f4aa9"}

The export produces a JSON file **per patient**.\n\n**Data sections documented with field-level detail:**\n\n| Section | Fields Documented | Description |\n|---------|-------------------|-------------|\n| **Patient** | 13 fields | Demographics: PatientId, PatientName, ChartID, DOB, Case (service line), Address, City, State, Zip, Country, HomePhone, WorkPhone, MobilePhone, Email |\n| **KinList** | 10 fields | Caregivers/relatives: PatientId, KinId, FirstName, LastName, MobilePhone, Email, DeceasedFlag, Decisional, Country, RelationshipList |\n| **Meds** | 13 fields | Medications: Strength, StrengthUnit, Form, DoseQuantity, StrengthForm, StartDate, EndDate, DoseUnit, Drug, DrugId, DrugInstructions, WrittenAs, Status |\n| **Allergies** | 4 fields | Allergen, Reaction, WrittenAs, Status |\n| **Problems** | 6 fields | ClinicalSummaryProblemDetailsList: DictionaryCode (ICD-10), Problems (description), ActiveDate, ResolvedDate, ProblemId, isResolved, isPrimaryDx |\n\n**Sample JSON export** is included (pages 7–9), showing a complete patient record with all five sections populated. The sample reveals additional undocumented structures:\n\n- A **CaseList** array containing case/encounter-level data: provider information (ProviderId, ProviderName), patient location details, case descriptions (e.g., \"Hospice\"), diagnosis codes, insurance lists, external/internal contact lists, and creation timestamps.\n- The CaseList section includes a **CaseProvider** sub-object with provider identifiers and names.\n- The CaseList includes **InsuranceList**, **ExternalContactList**, and **InternalContactList** arrays (empty in the sample but present in the structure).\n\nThese CaseList fields are NOT documented in the field-level tables but ARE present in the sample JSON output.\n\nEach field has a name, data type (string, date, bool, int), and a brief description. Date formats are specified (e.g., \"m/d/yyyy\" for DOB, ISO 8601 timestamps in the sample data).\n\n### ONC Disclosure Statement\n\nA 2-page PDF listing all certified criteria for SMARTMD Palliative Version 6. Confirms (b)(10) certification. Notes that EMR Direct Interoperability Engine v2023 is relied-upon software for (b)(1) and (h)(1). Updated January 25, 2025.\n\n### FHIR API Documentation\n\nA 58-page PDF documenting the SMART on FHIR API (for g(10) certification). This covers:\n- FHIR endpoints (production: https://fhir.smartmd.com:9443/fhir-server/api/v4/)\n- SMART on FHIR authentication (OAuth2, symmetric/asymmetric/public client flows)\n- FHIR resources: Patient, AllergyIntolerance, CarePlan, CareTeam, Condition, ImplantableDevice, DiagnosticReport/ClinicalNotes/DocumentReference, Laboratory Observations, Goal, Immunization, Medication, SmokingStatus, Procedure, Provenance, VitalSigns, CCDA\n- Search operations (Patient, Encounter, Document)\n\nThis is the **g(10) standardized API** documentation, NOT the b(10) EHI export. It covers USCDI v1 data classes via FHIR R4. The b(10) export is the separate JSON-based mechanism described in the EHI export PDF.\n\n### API.json\n\nA FHIR R4 Bundle (collection) containing two Endpoint resources and two Organization resources. These are Direct messaging endpoint configurations pointing to a test/certification server (fhirserver.justtest.in), not production endpoints. This appears to be related to the (h)(1) Direct Project criterion rather than the b(10) export.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nBased on the product research, SMARTMD Palliative stores data across these domains. Here is how the b(10) export covers them:\n\n| Data Domain | Covered in Export? | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Patient demographics** | Yes | 13 fields in Patient section |\n| **Next of kin / caregivers** | Yes | KinList section with contacts and decisional status |\n| **Medications** | Yes | Active medications with drug details, dosing, start/end dates |\n| **Allergies** | Yes | Drug/food/environmental allergies with reactions |\n| **Problems / Diagnoses** | Yes | ICD-10 coded, with onset/resolution dates, primary dx flag |\n| **Provider assignments** | Partial | In CaseList sample but undocumented |\n| **Case/service line info** | Partial | In CaseList sample but undocumented |\n| **Insurance** | Partial | InsuranceList array present in sample JSON but empty; no field docs |\n| **Clinical encounter notes** | **Not documented** | Core product feature — palliative care notes, dictation transcriptions |\n| **Palliative assessments (PPS, FAST, ESAS)** | **Not documented** | Specialty-specific structured assessments are a key product feature |\n| **Billing / charges / CPT codes** | **Not documented** | Time-based chronic care billing (PCM, TCM, CCM) is a core module |\n| **Scheduled appointments** | **Not documented** | Provider scheduling with mobile push |\n| **Secure messages** | **Not documented** | HIPAA-compliant team messaging |\n| **Scanned documents / images** | **Not documented** | Consent forms, scanned documents |\n| **Care plans** | **Not documented** | Implied by palliative workflows and CMS GUIDE model support |\n| **Quality measure data (PPS trends, acuity scores)** | **Not documented** | Extracted from notes and plotted as trend graphs |\n| **CRM / referral source data** | **Not documented** | May be a separate product (Accelerate CRM) |\n| **MIPS/quality reporting data** | **Not documented** | Registry and reporting capability |\n\n**The export covers approximately 5 of 15+ data domains** stored by the product. The documented export is essentially a patient demographic summary with medication, allergy, and problem lists — very similar to what a C-CDA patient summary or USCDI core dataset would include, just in JSON format.\n\n**Critical missing domains for a b(10) export:**\n\n1. **Clinical encounter notes** — This is the product's primary function. Palliative care providers create detailed clinical documentation at every visit. No notes appear in the export.\n2. **Palliative care assessments** (PPS, FAST, ESAS) — These are structured, specialty-specific clinical instruments that are central to the product. They track patient trajectory toward hospice. Their absence is a significant gap.\n3. **Billing and charge data** — The product has detailed time-based billing with CPT code recommendations. No billing data appears in the export.\n4. **Care plans** — Implied by the palliative care workflow and CMS GUIDE model support. Not in the export.\n5. **Scanned documents and images** — Consent forms and other documents mentioned in app store listings. Not in the export.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **proprietary JSON format** — not FHIR, not C-CDA, not any recognized healthcare interoperability standard. This is not inherently problematic for b(10); a vendor-specific format is acceptable as long as it includes all EHI. However:\n\n- The JSON structure is flat and simple — good for basic parsing\n- Field naming is inconsistent (camelCase mixed with PascalCase)\n- Date formats vary within the same export (e.g., \"m/d/yyyy\" vs ISO 8601 timestamps)\n- The export is per-patient, which is appropriate for individual patient requests\n- A third party could reconstruct the demographic/medication/allergy/problem data from this export, but NOT the full patient record since clinical notes, assessments, billing, and documents are absent\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n- **Readable but minimal** — The 10-page PDF is clearly formatted with tables for each section. Field names, types, and descriptions are provided.\n- **Sample data included** — A complete JSON example with all sections populated helps developers understand the format.\n- **Undocumented structures** — The sample JSON contains a CaseList structure with provider info, location data, insurance, and contacts that is NOT described in the field documentation tables. This means the actual export may be slightly broader than the documentation suggests, but the documentation is incomplete.\n- **No schema file** — There is no JSON Schema, no OpenAPI spec, no machine-readable format definition. Only the PDF tables and sample.\n- **No value sets** — The documentation doesn't define valid values for fields like \"Case\" (service line), \"Status\", or relationship types. The sample shows examples but doesn't enumerate possibilities.\n- **No versioning** — Labeled \"2024v1\" but no changelog or versioning strategy documented.\n\nA developer could implement a basic import of this data, but would need to handle the undocumented CaseList fields and guess at value constraints. The documentation is adequate for a patient summary import but insufficient for reconstructing a full patient record.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Field-level with name, type, and brief description. Better than just listing table names, but lacking constraints, cardinality, and optionality.\n- **Coded fields**: ICD-10 codes documented for Problems; RxNorm codes appear in the sample (RxNtCode field) but are not documented in the field tables. No other coded field documentation.\n- **Relationships**: Implicit via PatientId linkage across sections. No formal relationship documentation.\n- **Cardinality**: Not specified. The sample suggests arrays for KinList, Meds, Allergies, Problems, and CaseList, but min/max counts are not documented.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nSMARTMD Palliative has done the minimum to check the b(10) certification box. They have a dedicated EHI export feature (not just repurposed FHIR API), which is good — it demonstrates awareness that b(10) is separate from g(10). The JSON-based per-patient export with field documentation and sample data shows genuine effort.\n\nHowever, the export covers only the narrowest slice of patient data: demographics, medications, allergies, and problems. This is roughly equivalent to a patient summary — the same data that g(10) already covers through the FHIR API. For a palliative care EHR whose primary value is in its clinical documentation, structured assessments (PPS, FAST, ESAS), and specialty billing, the absence of notes, assessments, billing data, care plans, documents, and messages means the export captures perhaps 20-30% of the EHI the product stores.\n\nThe documentation itself is adequate for what it covers but incomplete even for the data it does export (CaseList undocumented). The lack of a machine-readable schema and the absence of value set definitions further limit its utility.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL: https://www.smartmd.com/compliance/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (all content accessible via curl; WordPress static page)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (two links directly on the compliance page)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare present but no blocking)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- None. The page was straightforward, all links worked, all downloads were valid. No login walls, no JavaScript-dependent content for the EHI links.\n- The API.json file is hosted on a different domain (secure.smartmd.com) but was publicly accessible.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
